  "ps_injection_240": "The YARA rule \"ps_injection_240\" is designed to detect the use of the \"getdelegateforfunctionpointer\" method, which is indicative of process injection techniques in PowerShell scripts, specifically aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001.   "powershell_ms16135_exploit": "The YARA rule \"powershell_ms16135_exploit\" is designed to detect the presence of a PowerShell script that implements the MS16-135 exploit, which targets a privilege escalation vulnerability in Windows systems (CVE-2016-7255).   "ps_injection_254": "The YARA rule \"ps_injection_254\" is designed to detect process injection techniques, specifically targeting the use of the \"NtCreateThreadEx\" function, which is commonly associated with creating remote threads in another process to execute malicious code.   "invoke_credentialinjection": "The YARA rule \"invoke_credentialinjection\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-credentialinjection\" technique, which is associated with the execution of commands and scripts via PowerShell (MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001). This technique is often used by threat actors to inject credentials into a system to gain unauthorized access or escalate privileges.",
  "invoke_eventhunter": "The YARA rule \"invoke_eventhunter\" is designed to detect the use of the PowerShell command \"invoke-eventhunter,\" which is associated with the execution of scripts or commands via PowerShell, a technique categorized under MITRE ATT&CK's T1059.001. This detection targets potential malicious activity where adversaries leverage PowerShell for executing unauthorized or harmful operations within a system.",
  "ps_windows_enumerate_com_objects_in_registry_with_powershell": "The YARA rule \"ps_windows_enumerate_com_objects_in_registry_with_powershell\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that enumerate COM objects within the Windows Registry, specifically targeting the HKEY_CLASSES_ROOT hive to extract CLSIDs and their associated methods.   "arpscanner_detection": "The YARA rule \"arpscanner_detection\" is designed to identify the execution of a PowerShell-based ARP scanning tool, which is indicative of network reconnaissance activities aimed at mapping and identifying devices within a local network. This behavior aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for malicious purposes, potentially facilitating lateral movement or further network exploitation.",
  "ip_address_url_obfuscator_script": "The YARA rule \"ip_address_url_obfuscator_script\" is designed to detect scripts that obfuscate URLs using IP addresses, potentially to evade security mechanisms and facilitate malicious activities such as phishing or unauthorized access. It targets the technique of obfuscating URLs by converting special characters to hexadecimal and manipulating URI schemes, as indicated by MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which pertains to command and scripting interpreter abuse.",
  "malicious_screenshot_capture": "The YARA rule \"malicious_screenshot_capture\" is designed to detect the execution of a PowerShell script that captures screenshots using the `.copyfromscreen` method, potentially exfiltrates them via a URL containing \"https://raw.githubusercontent\", and saves them to a suspicious location such as \"c:\\\\windows\\\\temp\\\\secret\", indicating unauthorized surveillance activity. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of scripting for malicious purposes, specifically highlighting the threat of data collection and exfiltration through PowerShell-based screenshot capture.",
  "portscan_tool_detection_3b081": "The YARA rule \"portscan_tool_detection_3b081\" is designed to identify the presence of a PowerShell-based port scanning tool, which is indicative of adversaries leveraging PowerShell scripts to perform network reconnaissance and identify open ports on target systems. This detection aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, highlighting the use of PowerShell for malicious scripting activities.",
  "malicious_powershell_netcat": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_netcat\" is designed to detect the execution of a PowerShell script that utilizes Netcat (nc.exe) for potentially malicious purposes, such as establishing unauthorized network connections or data exfiltration. It identifies specific command-line patterns associated with PowerShell's execution of Netcat, leveraging techniques related to MITRE ATT&CK's T1059.001, which involves scripting for execution.",
  "malicious_powershell_netscript": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_netscript\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that potentially engage in malicious network communication by overriding default certificate validation policies, specifically using ServicePointManager to trust all certificates, and employing Invoke-WebRequest with basic parsing to execute web requests. This behavior is indicative of the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of scripting for execution, often to facilitate unauthorized data exfiltration or command-and-control communication while bypassing security controls.",
  "suspicious_powershell_namedpipe": "The YARA rule \"suspicious_powershell_namedpipe\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize named pipes for inter-process communication, which may indicate lateral movement or command and control activities. The rule specifically targets patterns associated with the creation and management of named pipes, such as \"poshmsdaisy\" and \"pipeaccessrule,\" which align with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, highlighting the use of scripting for execution.",
  "generic_powershell_1a60e_367": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_1a60e_367\" is designed to detect the use of the \"invoke-urbanbishop\" command, which is associated with PowerShell-based code execution techniques. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, indicating its focus on identifying potential malicious script execution within PowerShell environments.",
  "powershell_persistence": "The YARA rule \"powershell_persistence\" is designed to detect malicious PowerShell scripts that establish persistence by utilizing the startup folder, a technique often associated with adversaries attempting to maintain access to a compromised system. The rule identifies specific strings indicative of suspicious behavior, such as disabling command logging and creating scripts that ensure the persistence of malicious processes, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution.",
  "ps_uac_bypass_eventvwr": "The YARA rule \"ps_uac_bypass_eventvwr\" is designed to detect a User Account Control (UAC) bypass technique that leverages the Windows Event Viewer (eventvwr.msc) to execute arbitrary commands with elevated privileges by modifying the registry key \"hkcu:\\\\software\\\\classes\\\\mscfile\\\\shell\\\\open\\\\command\" to point to \"c:\\\\windows\\\\system32\\\\cmd.exe\". This technique is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK tactic T1059.001, which involves command and scripting interpreter abuse for privilege escalation.",
  "jaws_enumeration_script_detection": "The \"jaws_enumeration_script_detection\" YARA rule is designed to identify the execution of the JAWS enumeration script, which is used for privilege escalation by gathering system and user information, checking for disabled firewalls, and searching for stored credentials and system files containing passwords. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell scripts for malicious purposes, specifically focusing on the enumeration and exploitation of system vulnerabilities to elevate privileges.",
  "malicious_powershell_process_kill": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_process_kill\" is designed to detect a PowerShell script exhibiting malicious behavior by continuously terminating other PowerShell processes, potentially indicating a denial-of-service attack or an attempt to disrupt legitimate system operations. This activity aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the abuse of PowerShell for executing malicious commands and scripts.",
  "outchm_chmmalware": "The YARA rule \"outchm_chmmalware\" is designed to detect a PowerShell script that generates a malicious Compiled HTML Help (CHM) file, leveraging techniques such as downloading payloads via `net.webclient`, executing scripts with hidden windows and bypassing execution policies, and potentially using Nishang's `Invoke-Encode` for obfuscation. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the abuse of PowerShell for execution, indicating a threat related to script-based malware delivery and execution.",
  "blindghost_powershell_script": "The YARA rule \"blindghost_powershell_script\" is designed to detect malicious activity associated with the execution of a PowerShell script that employs LLMNR/NBT-NS spoofing techniques to identify and exploit live hosts, potentially indicating lateral movement or credential theft attempts.   "maliciouspowershell_tcpclient": "The YARA rule \"maliciouspowershell_tcpclient\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that utilize the .NET TcpClient class to establish network connections, potentially indicating malicious activity such as unauthorized data exfiltration or remote command execution.   "invoke_ms16_135": "The YARA rule \"invoke_ms16_135\" is designed to detect the presence of scripts or code that attempt to exploit the MS16-135 vulnerability, which is associated with remote code execution in Microsoft Windows systems.   "ps_art_ttp_16": "The YARA rule \"ps_art_ttp_16\" is designed to detect the presence of a custom shell host executable, \"customshellhost.exe,\" which is indicative of the use of a custom command and scripting interpreter, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001. This rule targets the identification of potential malicious activity involving the execution of scripts or commands through non-standard interpreters, which may be used by threat actors to evade detection and execute unauthorized actions on a compromised system.",
  "get_amsiscanresult_script": "The YARA rule \"get_amsiscanresult_script\" is designed to detect the execution of a PowerShell script that attempts to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) by modifying its behavior, potentially allowing malicious scripts to execute without being detected by security software.   "suspicious_psexec_use": "The YARA rule \"suspicious_psexec_use\" is designed to detect the execution of a PowerShell script that leverages PsExec-like functionality to remotely create and start services on a target system, potentially indicating lateral movement or remote code execution as part of a cyber attack.   "invoke_packetknock": "The YARA rule \"invoke_packetknock\" is designed to detect the presence of a script or command execution that utilizes the term \"invoke-packetknock,\" which likely indicates the use of a packet knocking technique for covertly opening network ports.   "ps_microburst_azurekeyvaultsautomation": "The YARA rule \"ps_microburst_azurekeyvaultsautomation\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that automate interactions with Azure Key Vaults, potentially indicating unauthorized access or manipulation of sensitive cryptographic keys and secrets stored within the vaults.   "nettrace_enumeration": "The YARA rule \"nettrace_enumeration\" is designed to detect aggressive system enumeration activities using the \"netsh\" command, which may indicate an attempt to gather network configuration details and escalate privileges, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell).   "aduser_recon_activity": "The YARA rule \"aduser_recon_activity\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that perform reconnaissance activities on Active Directory user accounts, specifically targeting attributes related to password status and expiration.   "invoke_get_rbcd_threaded": "The YARA rule \"invoke_get_rbcd_threaded\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ techniques such as in-memory object creation, base64 decoding, and reflection to execute potentially malicious code, indicative of code injection or execution via scripting (MITRE ATT&CK Technique T1059.001).   "http_backdoor_nishang": "The YARA rule \"http_backdoor_nishang\" is designed to detect the presence and activity of a Nishang HTTP backdoor script, which is a PowerShell-based tool used for remote command execution and data exfiltration over HTTP.   "ps_obfuscation_283": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_283\" is designed to detect the use of obfuscated PowerShell scripts that attempt to open external resources via the \"workbooks.open('http\" pattern, indicating potential malicious activity related to the execution of scripts or commands through PowerShell, as categorized under MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell).   "stop_service_detection": "The YARA rule \"stop_service_detection\" identifies suspicious PowerShell activity aimed at stopping the Windows Defender service, which is indicative of an adversary attempting to disable security defenses on a system.   "generic_powershell_963fc_542": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_963fc_542\" is designed to detect the use of obfuscated PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-loadassemblyxor\" technique, which is indicative of code injection or execution of malicious payloads in memory.   "ps_obfuscation_282": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_282\" is designed to detect the use of obfuscated PowerShell commands, specifically targeting the technique of invoking workflows with encoded scripts, which is indicative of adversaries attempting to execute malicious code while evading detection.   "malicious_powershell_magicbypass": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_magicbypass\" is designed to detect the presence of potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that employ techniques to bypass security controls, specifically targeting the execution of unauthorized code by manipulating assemblies and methods within the current process, as indicated by the detection strings.   "psgetsys_process_injection": "The YARA rule \"psgetsys_process_injection\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that potentially perform process injection through parent process manipulation, a technique associated with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001.   "invoke_hostrecon": "The YARA rule \"invoke_hostrecon\" is designed to detect the execution of PowerShell scripts that utilize the \"invoke-hostrecon\" command, which is indicative of adversaries performing system discovery and reconnaissance activities on a compromised host.   "malicious_bind_shell_powershell": "The YARA rule \"malicious_bind_shell_powershell\" is designed to detect a PowerShell script that establishes a bind shell using the \"netsh hostednetwork\" feature, which allows network configuration and manipulation, potentially enabling unauthorized remote access.   "generic_powershell_acfcd_546": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_acfcd_546\" is designed to detect the use of a potentially malicious PowerShell script, specifically identified by the string \"invoke-p0wnedshellx86,\" which suggests the execution of unauthorized or harmful code via PowerShell.   "invoke_checkmouseinuse": "The YARA rule \"invoke_checkmouseinuse\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that utilize the \"invoke-checkmouseinuse\" function, which is indicative of adversaries employing scripting techniques to interact with or monitor mouse activity as part of their execution strategy.   "invoke_stealthuserhunter": "The YARA rule \"invoke_stealthuserhunter\" is designed to detect the presence of scripts or commands associated with the \"Invoke-StealthUserHunter\" technique, which is a PowerShell-based method for stealthily enumerating user sessions on a compromised system.   "ps_art_ttp_17": "The YARA rule \"ps_art_ttp_17\" is designed to detect malicious activity associated with the exfiltration of email data, specifically targeting the use of scripting to access and extract inbox contents into a file named \"mail.csv,\" aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (Command and Scripting Interpreter) and T1114.001 (Email Collection).   "ps_unmanaged_code_1000": "The YARA rule \"ps_unmanaged_code_1000\" is designed to detect the presence of unmanaged code execution within PowerShell scripts, specifically identifying the use of \"dllimport\" which indicates the invocation of external DLLs.   "amsi_bypass_detection_2d48c": "The YARA rule \"amsi_bypass_detection_2d48c\" is designed to identify attempts to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) by detecting specific strings associated with known techniques, such as invoking Rubeus commands or using scripts that contain \"amsi_lines -file\" and \"amsi_bypass.\" This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves PowerShell-based execution methods often used by adversaries to evade security mechanisms and execute malicious code.",
  "localbrute_forcepwddetection": "The YARA rule \"localbrute_forcepwddetection\" is designed to identify scripts that perform local brute-force password attacks by leveraging PowerShell commands and .NET assemblies to validate credentials against local accounts, as indicated by the presence of strings related to account password manipulation, assembly loading, and directory services.   "grayb0x_powershell_script": "The YARA rule \"grayb0x_powershell_script\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that perform network scanning and information gathering activities, indicative of adversarial reconnaissance and system enumeration.   "setmacattribute_malicioususage": "The YARA rule \"setmacattribute_malicioususage\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize the \"set-macattribute\" function, which may be indicative of attempts to manipulate file metadata attributes for evasion or persistence purposes.   "ps_amsi_bypass_244": "The YARA rule \"ps_amsi_bypass_244\" is designed to detect attempts to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) in PowerShell scripts, specifically by identifying the presence of the string \"amsiinitfailed,\" which is commonly used in techniques to disable or evade AMSI's scanning capabilities.   "suspected_malware_patch": "The YARA rule \"suspected_malware_patch\" is designed to detect potential malware that employs techniques to bypass or disable security mechanisms, specifically targeting the AMSI (Antimalware Scan Interface) by using functions such as \"getprocaddress\" and \"virtualprotect\" to manipulate memory and evade detection, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves PowerShell scripting for execution.   "powershell_inmemory_manipulation": "The YARA rule \"powershell_inmemory_manipulation\" is designed to detect techniques associated with in-memory manipulation of PowerShell processes, specifically targeting methods used to bypass or disable AMSI (Antimalware Scan Interface) and other security mechanisms.   "powershell_amsi_patch_58f90": "The YARA rule \"powershell_amsi_patch_58f90\" is designed to detect attempts to bypass or disable the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) in PowerShell scripts, a technique often used by adversaries to execute malicious code without detection.   "malicious_powershell_backdoor_5f6fa": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_backdoor_5f6fa\" is designed to detect a malicious PowerShell script that leverages OAuth2.0 authorization flows to illicitly obtain access tokens for Microsoft Graph API, potentially enabling unauthorized access to backdoored tenants.   "malicious_powershell_detection_c4a7d": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_detection_c4a7d\" is designed to identify potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that attempt to bypass or disable security mechanisms, such as AMSI (Antimalware Scan Interface), by using techniques like DLL imports and Base64 encoding. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for executing malicious code, often to evade detection and facilitate further exploitation.",
  "ps_token_manipulation_1065": "The YARA rule \"ps_token_manipulation_1065\" is designed to detect the use of the \"AdjustTokenPrivileges\" function, which is indicative of privilege escalation attempts where a process modifies its access token to gain elevated permissions.   "keylogger_detection_script": "The YARA rule \"keylogger_detection_script\" is designed to identify malicious activity associated with a PowerShell-based keylogger, which captures and logs keystrokes by utilizing functions such as \"getasynckeystate\" and \"getkeyboardstate\" to monitor keyboard input, and \"appendalltext\" to store the captured data.   "malware_powershell_script": "The YARA rule \"malware_powershell_script\" is designed to detect malicious PowerShell scripts that exhibit behaviors associated with process injection and manipulation, as indicated by the presence of strings related to memory handling and date retrieval functions.   "ps_token_manipulation_1071": "The YARA rule \"ps_token_manipulation_1071\" is designed to detect the use of the \"openprocesstoken\" function, which is indicative of a technique used to manipulate process tokens for privilege escalation or unauthorized access, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell).   "nishang_getinformation_detection": "The YARA rule \"nishang_getinformation_detection\" is designed to identify the execution of the Nishang \"Get-Information\" script, which is a PowerShell-based tool used for gathering detailed system and user information, potentially aiding in reconnaissance activities.   "fodhelper_uac_bypass_8a687": "The YARA rule \"fodhelper_uac_bypass_8a687\" is designed to detect attempts to bypass User Account Control (UAC) on Windows systems by exploiting the \"fodhelper.exe\" executable, a technique categorized under MITRE ATT&CK's \"Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell\" (T1059.001).   "generic_powershell_c2ea9_147": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_c2ea9_147\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that load shellcode, specifically identifying the invocation of a shellcode loader function, which is indicative of code injection or execution techniques commonly associated with adversary tactics for executing malicious payloads.   "ps_windows_windows_timestomp_a_file": "The YARA rule \"ps_windows_windows_timestomp_a_file\" is designed to detect the use of the \"timestomp\" technique, specifically targeting the manipulation of file timestamps on Windows systems, as indicated by the presence of the \"import-module\" command and the file \"kxwn.lock.\" This activity is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1070.006, which involves adversaries attempting to obscure their tracks by altering file metadata to hinder forensic analysis.",
  "reverseshell_detection_0e605": "The YARA rule \"reverseshell_detection_0e605\" is designed to detect the creation of a reverse shell using PowerShell, characterized by the instantiation of a TCP client object and manipulation of byte streams, which aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell).   "get_smbstatus_detection": "The \"get_smbstatus_detection\" YARA rule identifies potentially malicious PowerShell activity aimed at enumerating SMB protocol details, which may indicate lateral movement or reconnaissance efforts within a network.   "generic_powershell_fabbf_680": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_fabbf_680\" is designed to detect the use of the \"Invoke-BadPotato\" script, which is associated with privilege escalation through exploitation of Windows service permissions, specifically targeting the PowerShell scripting environment.   "captureserver_malicious_listener": "The YARA rule \"captureserver_malicious_listener\" is designed to detect a malicious PowerShell script that captures NTLM credentials by monitoring network traffic and logging authentication requests, indicative of credential theft or unauthorized access attempts.   "powershell_downgrade_attempt": "The YARA rule \"powershell_downgrade_attempt\" is designed to detect attempts to downgrade PowerShell versions or configurations to bypass security mechanisms such as Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI), which is indicative of adversaries employing the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell) to execute malicious scripts or commands.   "badusb_wificredentialextraction": "The YARA rule \"badusb_wificredentialextraction\" is designed to detect a potentially malicious PowerShell script that extracts Wi-Fi credentials from a system by utilizing the \"netsh wlan show profiles\" command to enumerate saved Wi-Fi profiles and retrieve their passwords, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for command and script execution.   "ps_caldera_ttp_8": "The YARA rule \"ps_caldera_ttp_8\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that exhibit behaviors associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the execution of commands and scripts via PowerShell.   "ps_caldera_ttp_10": "The YARA rule \"ps_caldera_ttp_10\" is designed to detect scripts or files that exhibit characteristics associated with PowerShell-based credential theft or manipulation, as indicated by the presence of strings related to credential storage and session management.   "malicious_network_drive_mapping": "The YARA rule \"malicious_network_drive_mapping\" is designed to detect the suspicious mapping of network drives, specifically targeting the use of the \"net use\" command to map local administrative shares (e.g., \"c$\") to the loopback address (127.0.0.1), which may indicate lateral movement or unauthorized access attempts within a network.   "ps_hyperv_atomic_vm": "The YARA rule \"ps_hyperv_atomic_vm\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell commands related to the creation and manipulation of Hyper-V virtual machines, which may indicate the execution of a technique associated with MITRE ATT&CK's T1059.001, PowerShell.   "potentially_malicious_powershell_addition": "The YARA rule \"potentially_malicious_powershell_addition\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that may be used to escalate privileges by adding users to local or domain administrator groups, potentially indicating unauthorized access or lateral movement within a network.   "ligolo_agent_injection": "The YARA rule \"ligolo_agent_injection\" is designed to detect a PowerShell script that performs process injection by reading shellcode into memory and attempting to inject it into a running process, indicative of the execution of malicious payloads. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for scripting and automation to facilitate unauthorized code execution and memory manipulation.",
  "invoke_passwordsprayowa": "The YARA rule \"invoke_passwordsprayowa\" is designed to detect the use of a PowerShell script that performs password spraying attacks against Outlook Web Access (OWA) services, a technique categorized under MITRE ATT&CK's T1059.001 for Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell. This rule identifies attempts to exploit weak passwords across multiple accounts, potentially leading to unauthorized access to email systems.",
  "invoke_lockless_maliciousscript": "The YARA rule \"invoke_lockless_maliciousscript\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize the \"invoke-lockless\" technique, which involves the use of obfuscation and in-memory execution to evade detection. This rule specifically targets scripts that employ base64 encoding, memory manipulation, and dynamic assembly loading, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which pertains to the execution of scripts for malicious purposes.",
  "internalmonologue_detection": "The YARA rule \"internalmonologue_detection\" is designed to identify the use of the Internal Monologue attack method, which involves the extraction of NetNTLM hashes from memory without sending network traffic, typically by leveraging techniques such as Base64 decoding and dynamic DLL loading. This detection is associated with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, indicating the use of PowerShell for execution, and targets credential access and lateral movement within compromised systems.",
  "pester_powershell_script": "The YARA rule \"pester_powershell_script\" is designed to detect the presence of PowerShell scripts utilizing the Pester testing framework, which may be leveraged by adversaries to execute malicious code or perform unauthorized actions under the guise of legitimate testing activities, corresponding to the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell). The detection strings indicate the rule targets specific Pester command patterns that could be indicative of script-based attacks or exploitation attempts.",
  "registryedit_remoteexecution": "The YARA rule \"registryedit_remoteexecution\" is designed to detect suspicious PowerShell activity indicative of remote execution and registry manipulation, specifically targeting behaviors associated with executing payloads remotely, invoking processes via rundll32, and editing registry entries to facilitate unauthorized remote process execution. This rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for malicious purposes, including remote code execution and system configuration changes.",
  "powershell_suspiciousassertion": "The YARA rule \"powershell_suspiciousassertion\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize assertion functions, which may indicate the presence of automated testing frameworks being misused for executing unauthorized or harmful code. This rule targets scripts that employ specific assertion-related functions, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the abuse of scripting languages for execution.",
  "chalumeau_malicious_powershell": "The YARA rule \"chalumeau_malicious_powershell\" is designed to detect the execution of a malicious PowerShell script associated with the Chalumeau malware, which is used to exfiltrate credentials to a command and control (C2) server. This rule specifically targets the identification of script patterns indicative of credential theft and unauthorized data transmission, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which pertains to the use of PowerShell for malicious purposes.",
  "verify_false_malicious": "The YARA rule \"verify_false_malicious\" is designed to detect the presence of a potentially malicious PowerShell function that manipulates boolean values, specifically identifying scripts that output unexpected results by comparing `$false` with an actual value, indicative of obfuscation or logic manipulation techniques. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, often leveraged by threat actors to execute malicious payloads or evade detection.",
  "reverseshell_detection_86a43": "The YARA rule \"reverseshell_detection_86a43\" is designed to identify potential reverse shell activity within PowerShell scripts by detecting specific patterns indicative of network connections and data transmission, such as the instantiation of TCP client objects and stream writing operations.   "generic_powershell_a7bd9_633": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_a7bd9_633\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-checkwrite\" function, which is indicative of potential file manipulation or privilege escalation activities. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, highlighting the use of PowerShell for execution in a potentially malicious context.",
  "powershell_malware_detection_a4d33": "The YARA rule \"powershell_malware_detection_a4d33\" is designed to detect malicious PowerShell scripts that exhibit behaviors associated with the execution of potentially harmful commands and operations, such as network scanning, dynamic assembly manipulation, and registry modifications. Specifically, it targets scripts that utilize obfuscated or uncommon strings indicative of techniques related to MITRE ATT&CK's T1059.001, which involves the abuse of PowerShell for executing malicious payloads and facilitating lateral movement or persistence within a compromised environment.",
  "potentially_malicious_base64encoder": "The YARA rule \"potentially_malicious_base64encoder\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that utilize base64 encoding techniques, often indicative of obfuscation, to execute potentially malicious commands such as launching processes (e.g., \"calc.exe\") and manipulating system text encoding and conversion functions.   "invoke_netripper": "The YARA rule \"invoke_netripper\" is designed to detect the presence of the \"invoke-netripper\" string, which is associated with the execution of PowerShell scripts for malicious purposes, specifically targeting the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell). This rule identifies potential threats related to script-based execution that may be used for unauthorized access or data exfiltration activities.",
  "amsi_bypass_detection_065fe": "The YARA rule \"amsi_bypass_detection_065fe\" is designed to detect attempts to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) in Windows environments, specifically targeting scripts or binaries that contain strings indicative of AMSI evasion techniques, such as \"borrando clave\" and \"[+] bypass de amsi.\" This rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, highlighting a common method employed by adversaries to circumvent security controls and execute malicious code undetected.",
  "malicious_powershell_activity_cd1ab": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_activity_cd1ab\" is designed to detect potentially harmful PowerShell activities indicative of script-based attacks, specifically targeting techniques for evading detection and executing encoded or encrypted payloads, as referenced by MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001. The rule identifies suspicious patterns such as AMSI bypass attempts, manipulation of PowerShell settings, and the use of encoding and encryption functions, which are commonly associated with adversaries seeking to obfuscate and execute malicious code.",
  "ntds_dump_detection": "The YARA rule \"ntds_dump_detection\" is designed to identify malicious PowerShell scripts that attempt to exfiltrate Active Directory database information by leveraging the ntdsutil tool, a technique associated with credential dumping.   "malicious_powershell_farmer": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_farmer\" is designed to detect a potentially malicious PowerShell script named \"invoke-farmer,\" which employs techniques such as Base64 decoding and GZIP compression to obfuscate its payload, indicative of the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell). This rule targets scripts that execute a suspicious function \"[f4rm3r.program]::main,\" suggesting the presence of a custom or unauthorized program execution, which may be used for malicious activities such as data exfiltration or system compromise.",
  "powershell_spawn_process_under_different_parent": "The YARA rule \"powershell_spawn_process_under_different_parent\" is designed to detect malicious PowerShell scripts that attempt to spawn a process under a different parent process, potentially indicating privilege escalation or evasion techniques. This behavior aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for executing commands that manipulate process hierarchies to bypass security controls or gain unauthorized access.",
  "generic_powershell_a4fe9_725": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_a4fe9_725\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-smbexec\" command, which is indicative of lateral movement techniques leveraging SMB protocol for remote code execution, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001.   "suspicious_powershell_cimregistrykey": "The YARA rule \"suspicious_powershell_cimregistrykey\" is designed to detect the potential misuse of PowerShell's CIM (Common Information Model) methods, specifically targeting the \"invoke-cimmethod\" function, which may be used to query remote registry keys for unauthorized access or data exfiltration.   "generic_powershell_8a420_651": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_8a420_651\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-loader\" function, which is indicative of the execution of potentially malicious code or payloads via PowerShell, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 for Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell.   "invoke_executemsbuild_detection_352d8": "The YARA rule \"invoke_executemsbuild_detection_352d8\" is designed to identify the execution of PowerShell scripts through the misuse of MSBuild, a legitimate Microsoft tool, which is indicative of a technique used by adversaries to execute arbitrary code under the guise of a trusted process.   "haystackburn_detection": "The \"haystackburn_detection\" YARA rule is designed to identify PowerShell scripts that facilitate lateral movement and credential harvesting within a network, specifically targeting scripts that search for sensitive information such as connection strings and passwords, and potentially manipulate AWS credentials.   "generic_powershell_d08ae_726": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_d08ae_726\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that invoke Excel COM objects, a technique often associated with the execution of malicious code through Excel macros or scripts, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell).   "invoke_writepayloadtoad": "The YARA rule \"invoke_writepayloadtoad\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-writepayloadtoad\" function, which is indicative of a technique used to execute payloads in Active Directory environments.   "ps_art_ttp_5": "The YARA rule \"ps_art_ttp_5\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that leverage the technique of script execution via InstallUtil, as indicated by the MITRE ATT&CK tag T1059.001.   "generic_powershell_5464e_753": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_5464e_753\" is designed to detect the presence of potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize specific functions such as \"invoke-getrekt,\" \"invoke-stresscpu,\" and \"invoke-stressmem,\" which are indicative of post-exploitation activities aimed at system manipulation or resource exhaustion.   "malicious_powershell_computerdetail": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_computerdetail\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that perform system profiling by gathering detailed information about logon events, AppLocker processes, and Remote Desktop Protocol (RDP) client data, which aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 for Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell.   "malicious_powershell_deletelogs": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_deletelogs\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that aim to delete or manipulate event logs, which is indicative of an adversary attempting to cover their tracks or erase evidence of unauthorized activities.   "invoke_lateralmovement": "The YARA rule \"invoke_lateralmovement\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that facilitate lateral movement within a network, specifically targeting the technique identified by MITRE ATT&CK as T1059.001, which involves the execution of commands and scripts via PowerShell. This rule identifies the presence of the string \"invoke-lateralmovement,\" indicating potential attempts to move laterally across systems by leveraging PowerShell capabilities.",
  "invoke_envbypass_uac": "The YARA rule \"invoke_envbypass_uac\" is designed to detect scripts that attempt to bypass User Account Control (UAC) by modifying the Windows registry, specifically targeting the use of the \"SilentCleanup\" task to execute elevated commands without user consent. This technique is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK tactic T1059.001, which involves command and scripting interpreter abuse to escalate privileges or execute unauthorized actions.",
  "ps_etw_bypass_359": "The YARA rule \"ps_etw_bypass_359\" is designed to detect attempts to bypass Event Tracing for Windows (ETW) logging by identifying specific PowerShell command patterns that manipulate ETW providers, which is indicative of adversaries employing the PowerShell scripting technique associated with MITRE ATT&CK tactic T1059.001 (PowerShell).   "invoke_smbclient": "The YARA rule \"invoke_smbclient\" is designed to detect the use of the \"invoke-smbclient\" command, which is associated with the execution of PowerShell scripts for interacting with SMB (Server Message Block) services, potentially indicating lateral movement or unauthorized access within a network.   "ps_obfuscation_231": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_231\" is designed to detect obfuscated PowerShell scripts that utilize environment variable manipulation and string joining techniques to evade detection, specifically targeting the use of the \"env:comspec\" variable in combination with the \"join\" method to reconstruct command strings.   "ps_obfuscation_225": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_225\" is designed to detect obfuscated PowerShell commands that utilize the \"iex\" (Invoke-Expression) function in conjunction with environment variables, a technique commonly associated with script-based execution and evasion tactics.   "malicious_powershell_script_c15ca": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_script_c15ca\" is designed to detect obfuscated or potentially harmful PowerShell scripts that utilize .NET reflective loading techniques, indicated by patterns such as \"intptr *(intptr hmodule\" and \"public class a,\" which are commonly associated with in-memory execution of malicious code.   "generic_powershell_51e03_49": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_51e03_49\" is designed to detect the use of the \"Invoke-Mimikittenz\" script, which is associated with credential theft and memory scraping activities executed via PowerShell.   "dsaccount_secretsdump_detection": "The YARA rule \"dsaccount_secretsdump_detection\" is designed to identify activity related to the use of the SecretsDump tool, which is commonly employed for extracting password hashes and other sensitive information from Active Directory databases, specifically targeting custom views associated with domain accounts.   "reverse_tcp_shell_detection": "The YARA rule \"reverse_tcp_shell_detection\" is designed to identify scripts that facilitate reverse TCP shell connections, a technique commonly used by attackers to gain remote control over a compromised system by establishing a connection from the victim's machine to an external server, as indicated by MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell). The detection strings suggest the rule targets scripts that execute commands received from a server, often encoded in base64, and manage connection states, which are indicative of remote command execution and control activities.",
  "ps_art_ttp_98": "The YARA rule \"ps_art_ttp_98\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell commands that modify the Windows Registry to alter the Winlogon shell configuration, specifically appending \"cmd.exe\" to \"explorer.exe,\" which is indicative of a persistence mechanism used by adversaries to execute command-line scripts upon user login.   "malicious_powershell_script_detection_44cb0": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_script_detection_44cb0\" is designed to identify the execution of PowerShell scripts that exhibit behaviors indicative of potential malicious activity, such as downloading files from the internet, executing a specific executable, checking for the existence of a file, and querying running processes.   "malicious_powershell_check": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_check\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell script activity associated with the abuse of PowerShell for executing commands that may indicate software package manipulation, version control tampering, or file system alterations, as evidenced by the presence of strings such as \"nuget.exe pack,\" \"git.exe describe,\" and \"remove-item -recurse.\" This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for executing scripts that could facilitate unauthorized access or system modifications.",
  "ps_caldera_ttp_12": "The YARA rule \"ps_caldera_ttp_12\" is designed to detect attempts to query Windows registry keys for strings containing \"password\" using the \"reg query\" command, which is indicative of credential harvesting or reconnaissance activities targeting sensitive information stored in the registry.   "invoke_netsessionenum": "The YARA rule \"invoke_netsessionenum\" is designed to detect the use of the PowerShell command \"invoke-netsessionenum,\" which is associated with the enumeration of network sessions on a Windows system, a technique often used by attackers for lateral movement and discovery of active sessions.   "ps_caldera_ttp_13": "The YARA rule \"ps_caldera_ttp_13\" is designed to detect malicious behavior associated with the execution of PowerShell scripts that leverage the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell) to download and execute cryptocurrency mining software, such as \"xmrig,\" from GitHub repositories.   "localbrute_bruteforce_detection": "The YARA rule \"localbrute_bruteforce_detection\" is designed to identify the execution of a local brute force script that leverages directory services for account validation, specifically targeting unauthorized access attempts by systematically trying multiple passwords.   "generic_powershell_baee3_73": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_baee3_73\" is designed to detect the use of specific PowerShell scripts associated with post-exploitation activities, which are indicative of adversaries leveraging PowerShell for stealthy reconnaissance and process manipulation.   "ps_art_ttp_99": "The YARA rule \"ps_art_ttp_99\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell commands that modify the Windows Registry to alter the Userinit value under the Winlogon key, potentially enabling the execution of cmd.exe upon user login.   "ps_art_ttp_66": "The YARA rule \"ps_art_ttp_66\" is designed to detect the presence of the executable \"injectview.exe,\" which is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, indicating potential use of PowerShell for script execution.   "patchamsi_current_process_detection": "The YARA rule \"patchamsi_current_process_detection\" is designed to identify attempts to bypass or disable the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) within the current PowerShell process, a technique commonly used by adversaries to evade detection of malicious scripts and payloads.   "invoke_userhunter": "The YARA rule \"invoke_userhunter\" is designed to detect the execution of a PowerShell script associated with the technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for malicious purposes, such as credential harvesting or lateral movement within a network.   "ad_connect_credential_extractor": "The YARA rule \"ad_connect_credential_extractor\" is designed to detect the execution of a PowerShell script that attempts to extract credentials from Microsoft Azure AD Connect by identifying specific strings associated with accessing the local database and executing commands via xp_cmdshell. This activity aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for malicious purposes, specifically targeting credential access and potential lateral movement within a network.",
  "ps_obfuscation_230": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_230\" is designed to detect obfuscated PowerShell commands that manipulate the environment variable \"PSHOME\" using specific indexing and concatenation patterns, indicative of script obfuscation techniques.   "disable_windows_defender_service": "The YARA rule \"disable_windows_defender_service\" is designed to detect malicious behavior involving the use of a PowerShell script to disable the Windows Defender service, which is indicative of an adversary attempting to weaken system defenses by leveraging the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell).   "suspicious_powershell_proxy": "The YARA rule \"suspicious_powershell_proxy\" is designed to detect potentially malicious activity involving the use of PowerShell to configure proxy settings for web requests, which may indicate attempts to obfuscate or redirect network traffic through a proxy to evade detection. This rule targets the technique of using PowerShell commands to manipulate web client and proxy objects, as outlined in MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which is associated with scripting for execution and lateral movement.",
  "ps_windows_enumerate_users_not_requiring_pre_auth_asreproast_": "The YARA rule \"ps_windows_enumerate_users_not_requiring_pre_auth_asreproast_\" is designed to detect the enumeration of Active Directory user accounts that do not require Kerberos pre-authentication, a technique often leveraged in AS-REP Roasting attacks. This technique, associated with MITRE ATT&CK ID T1069.002, allows attackers to obtain password hashes for offline cracking, potentially leading to unauthorized access to network resources.",
  "ps_browsercollector_activity": "The YARA rule \"ps_browsercollector_activity\" is designed to detect the presence and activity of a malicious program, \"browsercollector.exe,\" which targets web browsers to extract sensitive information such as stored passwords and login credentials, as indicated by the detection of files like \"key4.db\" and \"logins.json.\" This rule is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves command and scripting interpreter activity, and T1555.003, which pertains to credential dumping from web browsers.",
  "malicious_powershell_getcimuserassist": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_getcimuserassist\" is designed to detect suspicious PowerShell activity indicative of potential abuse of the Get-CimUserAssist cmdlet, which may involve unauthorized attempts to access user assist data using alternative authentication methods and implicit credentials, potentially exploiting the PowerShell environment for malicious purposes. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves command and scripting interpreter abuse, specifically focusing on PowerShell-based execution patterns that may compromise system security.",
  "ps_art_ttp_4": "The YARA rule \"ps_art_ttp_4\" is designed to detect the use of InstallUtil, a legitimate .NET framework tool, being leveraged for executing malicious code, specifically targeting the technique of \"Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell\" as indicated by MITRE ATT&CK tag T1059.001. The detection strings \"installutiltestharness\" and \"t1218.004.dll\" suggest the rule identifies attempts to abuse InstallUtil for executing unauthorized scripts or payloads, aligning with the defense evasion and execution tactics.",
  "defense_evasion_ttp": "The YARA rule \"defense_evasion_ttp\" is designed to detect scripts and commands indicative of defense evasion techniques, specifically targeting the execution of potentially malicious payloads and scripts in Windows environments. It identifies patterns associated with the use of tools and methods such as msfvenom payload generation, PowerShell execution bypass, and manipulation of system objects and memory allocation, which align with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell).",
  "ps_winapi_injection": "The YARA rule \"ps_winapi_injection\" is designed to detect malicious behavior associated with process injection techniques, specifically targeting the use of Windows API functions such as VirtualAlloc, WriteProcessMemory, and CreateRemoteThread, which are commonly employed by adversaries to execute arbitrary code within the address space of another process. This rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, indicating its focus on identifying script-based injection methods that facilitate unauthorized code execution and potential privilege escalation.",
  "malicious_pester_script_detection": "The YARA rule \"malicious_pester_script_detection\" is designed to identify potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that misuse the Pester testing framework, specifically targeting scripts that exhibit behaviors such as argument parsing, exception handling, and file content matching, which are indicative of adversaries leveraging PowerShell for execution (MITRE ATT&CK Technique T1059.001). This rule aims to detect scripts that may be used for unauthorized testing or validation of malicious code execution within a compromised environment.",
  "powershell_format_script": "The YARA rule \"powershell_format_script\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize suspicious formatting functions and module imports, which may indicate obfuscation or manipulation techniques commonly associated with adversary scripting activities. Specifically, it targets the use of unconventional formatting functions and module import patterns that align with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, highlighting potential script-based execution threats.",
  "ps_powerup_352": "The YARA rule \"ps_powerup_352\" is designed to detect the presence of PowerShell scripts or commands that utilize the technique of DLL hijacking, specifically targeting processes to execute unauthorized code, as indicated by the detection string \".hijackabledll.process.\" This rule is associated with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, highlighting its role in potentially facilitating privilege escalation or persistence through DLL manipulation.",
  "suspicious_powershell_clsid_report": "The YARA rule \"suspicious_powershell_clsid_report\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that are involved in extracting and processing CLSIDs (Class Identifiers) and AppIDs (Application Identifiers), which may indicate reconnaissance or enumeration activities associated with system components and COM objects. This behavior aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for potentially malicious purposes, such as gathering information for further exploitation or lateral movement within a network.",
  "generic_powershell_b149b_550": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_b149b_550\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that attempt to bypass User Account Control (UAC) by invoking the \"invoke-wscriptbypassuac\" method, which is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, indicating potential privilege escalation or unauthorized execution of elevated commands. This rule targets scripts that may be used by attackers to gain higher privileges on a Windows system without user consent.",
  "powershell_remoting_modification": "The YARA rule \"powershell_remoting_modification\" is designed to detect unauthorized modifications to PowerShell remoting security descriptors, which may indicate attempts to alter access controls or permissions for remote execution, potentially facilitating privilege escalation or unauthorized remote access. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for malicious purposes, specifically focusing on patterns that suggest manipulation of security settings to bypass standard security measures.",
  "powershell_tcpclient_detection": "The YARA rule \"powershell_tcpclient_detection\" is designed to identify the use of PowerShell scripts that leverage the .NET System.Net.Sockets.TcpClient class to establish network connections, potentially for malicious purposes such as remote command execution or data exfiltration, as indicated by the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell). The detection strings suggest the rule targets scripts that utilize methods for network communication and data manipulation, including GetStream, StreamWriter, and FromBase64String, which are commonly associated with covert data transmission and command-and-control activities.",
  "generic_powershell_ea0f5_224": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_ea0f5_224\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-downloadcradle\" technique, which is indicative of command and scripting interpreter abuse for downloading and executing potentially malicious payloads. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, highlighting the threat of adversaries leveraging PowerShell for remote code execution and lateral movement.",
  "malicious_powerview_script": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powerview_script\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell activity associated with the use of PowerView, a tool commonly employed for reconnaissance and privilege escalation in Active Directory environments. By identifying specific command patterns such as \"get-domaingroupmember\" and \"invoke-kerberoast,\" the rule targets techniques related to credential access and lateral movement, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which pertains to PowerShell-based execution.",
  "malicious_powershell_communication": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_communication\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize pipe communication and encryption techniques, often indicative of command and control (C2) activities or data exfiltration. By identifying specific strings such as \"microsoft error: \", \"[system.text.encoding]\", and \"waiting for client\", the rule targets scripts that may be attempting to obfuscate or encrypt data during unauthorized communication, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves PowerShell-based execution.",
  "potential_malicious_processmoduletrace": "The YARA rule \"potential_malicious_processmoduletrace\" is designed to detect potentially malicious activities involving process module tracing, specifically targeting the abuse of Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI) for event registration and module loading, as indicated by strings such as \"register-wmievent\" and \"win32_moduleloadtrace.\" This rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of scripting for execution, highlighting the threat of adversaries leveraging WMI to execute scripts or commands for unauthorized module tracing and loading.",
  "invoke_removecertificates": "The YARA rule \"invoke_removecertificates\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that invoke the \"Remove-Certificates\" command, which may be employed by adversaries to remove or manipulate digital certificates on a compromised system. This activity is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, indicating the use of PowerShell for execution, potentially facilitating unauthorized access or evasion of security mechanisms.",
  "generic_powershell_ebf43_480": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_ebf43_480\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ potentially malicious functions such as \"invoke-removecertificates,\" \"invoke-shellcode,\" and \"invoke-createcertificate,\" which are indicative of code execution and certificate manipulation activities. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for executing commands and scripts that may facilitate unauthorized access or system compromise.",
  "malicious_dotnet_powershell_script": "The YARA rule \"malicious_dotnet_powershell_script\" is designed to detect suspicious PowerShell activity involving .NET assemblies, specifically targeting techniques that manipulate or suppress assembly attributes, such as \"suppressildasmattr,\" which may indicate attempts to obfuscate or conceal malicious code execution. This rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, highlighting the use of PowerShell for executing potentially harmful scripts that interact with .NET components.",
  "powershell_tcp_reverse_shell": "The YARA rule \"powershell_tcp_reverse_shell\" is designed to detect malicious activity involving the use of PowerShell scripts to establish a TCP reverse shell, which allows an attacker to remotely execute commands on a compromised system by connecting back to the attacker's server. This rule targets specific strings and patterns associated with the creation and operation of reverse shells using PowerShell, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which pertains to command and scripting interpreter abuse.",
  "nonmalicious_powershell_scriptdetection": "The YARA rule \"nonmalicious_powershell_scriptdetection\" is designed to identify PowerShell scripts that utilize the Pester testing framework, specifically looking for non-malicious activities such as setting up test environments and manipulating test files and directories. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of scripting for legitimate purposes, distinguishing benign testing scripts from potentially harmful script-based attacks.",
  "port_scan_tcp_detection": "The YARA rule \"port_scan_tcp_detection\" is designed to identify malicious activity involving PowerShell scripts that perform TCP port scanning, a technique often used by attackers to discover open ports and services on target systems, which is categorized under MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell). The detection strings indicate the rule targets scripts that utilize .NET objects and asynchronous socket connections to execute port scanning operations, potentially indicating reconnaissance or lateral movement attempts.",
  "reverseshell_detection_rule": "The YARA rule \"reverseshell_detection_rule\" is designed to identify PowerShell scripts that potentially establish a reverse shell connection by utilizing the .NET `System.Net.Sockets.TcpClient` class to connect back to an attacker's server, often executed through the `Invoke-Expression` cmdlet. This detection aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of scripting for command and control purposes, indicating a potential remote access threat.",
  "add_scrnsavebackdoor_detection": "The YARA rule \"add_scrnsavebackdoor_detection\" identifies the malicious behavior of creating a backdoor by modifying registry keys associated with screensaver settings and employing debugger hijacking techniques, which can allow unauthorized execution of code under the guise of legitimate processes. This detection targets the abuse of PowerShell commands and registry manipulation to bypass execution policies and establish persistence, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell).",
  "powershell_malware_detection_99b6d": "The YARA rule \"powershell_malware_detection_99b6d\" is designed to detect malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize reflective loading and dynamic code execution techniques, as indicated by the presence of patterns such as \"currentdomain.getassemblies\" and \"definedynamicassembly.\" This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for executing code in a manner that may bypass traditional security controls, often associated with fileless malware and advanced persistent threats (APTs).",
  "malicious_powershell_sqlcmd": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_sqlcmd\" is designed to detect the execution of potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize SQL commands to perform unauthorized operations, specifically targeting the abuse of SQL Server's xp_cmdshell feature to execute system commands. This rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves command and scripting interpreter abuse, indicating a threat actor's attempt to leverage SQL Server permissions for lateral movement or system compromise.",
  "ps_base64_encode_1047": "The YARA rule \"ps_base64_encode_1047\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell commands that utilize base64 encoding, a technique often employed by attackers to obfuscate malicious scripts and evade detection. This rule specifically targets the \"encodedcommand\" parameter, which is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, indicating the use of command and scripting interpreters for execution.",
  "malicious_powershell_messagebox": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_messagebox\" is designed to detect the misuse of PowerShell scripts that employ message boxes, specifically identifying scripts that contain the strings \"my message box,\" \"testing message,\" and \"wscript.shell,\" which may indicate the execution of potentially harmful scripts using the Windows Script Host. This rule targets the technique of Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell (MITRE ATT&CK T1059.001), where adversaries might leverage PowerShell to execute scripts with malicious parameters under the guise of benign message box functions.",
  "ps_pastebin_1161": "The YARA rule \"ps_pastebin_1161\" is designed to detect the use of Pastebin, a popular web service for sharing text, as a medium for executing or hosting malicious scripts, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which pertains to command and scripting interpreter usage. This rule specifically targets the presence of \"pastebin.com\" within files or network traffic, indicating potential misuse for script-based attacks or data exfiltration.",
  "ps_microburst_msoldomaininfo": "The YARA rule \"ps_microburst_msoldomaininfo\" is designed to detect the use of the PowerShell command \"get-msoldomaininfo,\" which is associated with the enumeration of Microsoft Online domains, potentially indicating reconnaissance activity by an attacker. This behavior aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for malicious purposes, such as gathering information about domain configurations in a cloud environment.",
  "ps_caldera_3": "The YARA rule \"ps_caldera_3\" is designed to detect PowerShell-based malicious activity associated with the execution of scripts that download and execute files from a remote server, potentially indicating a fileless malware attack or command and control communication, as characterized by MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell). The rule identifies patterns related to web client operations, file manipulation, and process execution, which are indicative of adversaries leveraging PowerShell to stealthily download and execute payloads while maintaining a hidden window style to evade detection.",
  "powerexploit_installer_detection": "The YARA rule \"powerexploit_installer_detection\" is designed to identify the installation script of the PowerExploit pentesting framework, which is indicative of a potential threat actor attempting to deploy a post-exploitation toolkit. This detection focuses on the use of scripting techniques, specifically PowerShell, to download and install necessary components, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell).",
  "ps_microburst_azpasswords": "The YARA rule \"ps_microburst_azpasswords\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that attempt to retrieve Azure passwords, specifically identifying the invocation of the \"get-azpasswords\" command.   "powershell_invoke_shellcode": "The YARA rule \"powershell_invoke_shellcode\" is designed to detect the potentially malicious use of PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-shellcode\" function to execute shellcode within a process, often associated with techniques for process injection and remote access.   "ps_obfuscation_344": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_344\" is designed to detect PowerShell obfuscation techniques characterized by the repeated use of short variable substitutions, which is indicative of script obfuscation methods used to evade detection.   "get_cimlogonsession_detection": "The YARA rule \"get_cimlogonsession_detection\" is designed to identify the execution of the Get-CimLogonSession function, which is associated with the collection of logon session information from remote systems using CIM (Common Information Model) sessions. This detection is relevant to the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, indicating potential abuse of PowerShell for remote system reconnaissance and unauthorized access attempts, which may be indicative of lateral movement or credential theft activities.",
  "invoke_obfuscation_detection": "The YARA rule \"invoke_obfuscation_detection\" is designed to identify the use of the Invoke-Obfuscation tool, which is employed to obfuscate PowerShell scripts, potentially indicating an attempt to evade detection mechanisms by concealing malicious PowerShell activity.   "generic_powershell_9ccd6_528": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_9ccd6_528\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ techniques for decrypting data, bypassing security mechanisms such as AMSI (Antimalware Scan Interface), and potentially executing malicious payloads, as indicated by the presence of strings like \"invoke-aesdecrypt\" and \"invoke-amsibypass.\" This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, highlighting its role in facilitating post-exploitation activities and evading detection.",
  "ps_malicious_execution_319": "The YARA rule \"ps_malicious_execution_319\" is designed to detect the use of the Windows ShellExecute function, which is often leveraged by adversaries to execute commands or launch applications, potentially indicating malicious activity associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell).   "malicious_powershell_script_cfbac": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_script_cfbac\" is designed to detect the execution of potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that manipulate SQL server names, likely indicating an attempt to obfuscate or encode SQL-related operations, which aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell).   "encryptstringcmdletdetection": "The YARA rule \"encryptstringcmdletdetection\" is designed to identify the use of a PowerShell script that employs encryption techniques, specifically targeting the \"Encrypt-String\" cmdlet, which may be used to obfuscate data or facilitate secure data transmission.   "invoke_dcom_malware": "The YARA rule \"invoke_dcom_malware\" is designed to detect the use of the Invoke-Dcom PowerShell script, which facilitates lateral movement within a network by exploiting DCOM (Distributed Component Object Model) for remote code execution.   "ps_obfuscation_340": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_340\" is designed to detect obfuscated PowerShell scripts that utilize the \"foreach\" or \"foreach-object\" constructs to execute character-based obfuscation, a technique often employed to evade detection by converting integer values to characters.   "ms16_032_detection": "The YARA rule \"ms16_032_detection\" is designed to identify the execution of the MS16-032 exploit within PowerShell scripts, which targets a vulnerability in Windows to escalate privileges by leveraging specific strings indicative of the exploit's operation.   "malicious_powershell_badusb": "The YARA rule \"malicious_powershell_badusb\" is designed to detect a malicious PowerShell script indicative of a BadUSB attack, which leverages USB devices to execute unauthorized commands.   "pesterthrow_malicious_activity": "The YARA rule \"pesterthrow_malicious_activity\" is designed to detect PowerShell script activities related to exception handling and manipulation, specifically targeting behaviors associated with the abuse of exception messages to obfuscate or alter script execution flow.   "get_systeminfo_malicious_activity": "The YARA rule \"get_systeminfo_malicious_activity\" is designed to detect scripts that utilize PowerShell reflection techniques to load modules in memory for the purpose of retrieving system information, which is indicative of adversaries employing the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell) for reconnaissance or preparatory actions in a cyber attack.   "converttostring_maliciousdetection": "The YARA rule \"converttostring_maliciousdetection\" is designed to identify potentially malicious activity involving the conversion of binary data to string format, which may be indicative of script-based execution or manipulation of binary data using PowerShell, as referenced by MITRE technique T1059.001 (PowerShell).   "eventlogsuccess_malicious_activity": "The YARA rule \"eventlogsuccess_malicious_activity\" is designed to detect potentially malicious activity involving the use of PowerShell scripts that manipulate Windows Event Logs, specifically targeting successful logon events (Event ID 4624) with logon type 10, which indicates remote interactive logons.   "reverse_shell_sharpshell": "The YARA rule \"reverse_shell_sharpshell\" is designed to detect the presence and execution of a C#-based PowerShell reverse shell, specifically utilizing the SharpShell library, which is indicative of an adversary attempting to establish unauthorized remote control over a compromised system.   "powershell_fakebsod_prank": "The YARA rule \"powershell_fakebsod_prank\" is designed to detect a malicious PowerShell script that simulates a fake Blue Screen of Death (BSOD) as a prank, potentially causing disruption or panic among users.   "generic_powershell_ed29d_260": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_ed29d_260\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that invoke the \"invoke-sharphandler\" function, which is indicative of a technique associated with command and scripting interpreter abuse, specifically under MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001.   "suspicious_powershell_command": "The YARA rule \"suspicious_powershell_command\" is designed to detect potentially malicious execution of PowerShell commands that may involve remote command invocation, manipulation of secure strings, and interaction with system management components, which are indicative of adversaries leveraging PowerShell for execution and credential access as per MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001.   "generic_powershell_9b81f_15": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_9b81f_15\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ the technique of shellcode execution, as indicated by the presence of the string \"invoke-shellcodeexecution.\" This behavior is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for executing malicious code, potentially leading to unauthorized system access or control.",
  "ps_amsi_bypass_351": "The YARA rule \"ps_amsi_bypass_351\" is designed to detect attempts to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) in PowerShell scripts, a technique commonly used by attackers to evade detection and execute malicious code.   "ps_windows_system_discovery_using_sharpview": "The YARA rule \"ps_windows_system_discovery_using_sharpview\" is designed to detect the use of SharpView, a post-exploitation tool, to perform system discovery activities on Windows environments.   "invoke_dllinjection_powershell": "The YARA rule \"invoke_dllinjection_powershell\" is designed to detect malicious behavior involving the use of a PowerShell script to perform DLL injection into processes, a technique often used by attackers to execute arbitrary code within the address space of another process.   "powershell_udp_portscan": "The YARA rule \"powershell_udp_portscan\" is designed to detect scripts that perform UDP port scanning using PowerShell, which is indicative of network reconnaissance activities.   "invoke_powershelludp_detection": "The YARA rule \"invoke_powershelludp_detection\" is designed to identify the execution of the Invoke-PowerShellUdp script, which is indicative of potential reverse or bind shell activity over UDP, a technique associated with command and control operations.   "sdpropagator_tool": "The YARA rule \"sdpropagator_tool\" is designed to detect the execution of a PowerShell script known as \"sdpropagator,\" which is used to manipulate Active Directory security descriptors, potentially altering administrative group memberships and permissions.   "amsitamper_trojan_detection": "The YARA rule \"amsitamper_trojan_detection\" is designed to identify scripts that attempt to bypass or disable the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) in Windows environments, a technique commonly used by malware to evade detection.   "generic_powershell_9b6a9_189": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_9b6a9_189\" is designed to detect the presence of potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize specific function names indicative of unauthorized process manipulation, browser activity monitoring, task scheduling, data exfiltration to Pastebin, and media change detection.   "detect_potential_malicious_zip_functions": "The YARA rule \"detect_potential_malicious_zip_functions\" is designed to identify potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize custom zip file handling functions, which may indicate attempts to compress or extract files for unauthorized data exfiltration or lateral movement.   "amsi_bypass_powershell_ae766": "The YARA rule \"amsi_bypass_powershell_ae766\" is designed to detect PowerShell scripts that attempt to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) by utilizing unsafe native methods and specific function lookups, such as \"amsiopensession\" and \"lookupfunc amsi.dll,\" which are indicative of attempts to evade security scanning and execute potentially malicious code.   "powershell_malware_tool": "The YARA rule \"powershell_malware_tool\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that employ various obfuscation techniques, such as character, variable, ASCII, and base64 obfuscation, to conceal their payloads and evade detection.   "amsibypass_tool_detection": "The YARA rule \"amsibypass_tool_detection\" is designed to identify the use of a PowerShell-based tool that attempts to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) by manipulating its internal functions, such as \"amsiinitfailed\" and \"amsiscanbuffer,\" potentially using techniques like \"virtualprotect\" to alter memory protections.   "potential_terminal_server_registry_modification": "The YARA rule \"potential_terminal_server_registry_modification\" is designed to detect unauthorized modifications to the Windows registry settings related to terminal server connections, specifically targeting the \"fdenytsconnections\" value within the \"currentcontrolset\" registry path.   "ps_art_ttp_62": "The YARA rule \"ps_art_ttp_62\" is designed to detect malicious behavior associated with process injection techniques, specifically targeting the use of Windows API functions such as VirtualAlloc, VirtualProtect, RtlCopyMemory, and syscall, which are commonly employed by adversaries to execute arbitrary code within the address space of another process.   "generic_powershell_daf79_359": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_daf79_359\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that invoke the \"invoke-sharpdump\" command, which is indicative of credential dumping activities.
