  "generic_powershell_5cdd5_350": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_5cdd5_350\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that employ the \"invoke-convert\" function, which is indicative of command and scripting interpreter activity associated with the execution of potentially malicious scripts, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001. This rule targets the identification of scripts that may be used for unauthorized system access or manipulation, highlighting a common method for executing code in a Windows environment.",
  "invoke_sharpcloud_malicious": "The YARA rule \"invoke_sharpcloud_malicious\" is designed to detect potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize SharpCloud commands to perform suspicious activities such as memory manipulation and data decompression, indicative of code execution or data exfiltration techniques. Specifically, it targets behaviors associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of scripting for execution, highlighting potential threats related to unauthorized data access or manipulation.",
  "powershell_getvaultcredential": "The YARA rule \"powershell_getvaultcredential\" is designed to detect the execution of PowerShell scripts that access and enumerate Windows Vault credentials, potentially indicating credential theft or unauthorized access attempts. This rule targets the technique of using PowerShell to interact with Windows Vault, as categorized under MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves scripting for malicious purposes.",
  "getsystemmaliciousdetection": "The YARA rule \"getsystemmaliciousdetection\" is designed to identify the execution of the \"get-system\" function, which is often associated with privilege escalation techniques used by adversaries to gain elevated permissions on a compromised system. By detecting strings such as \"revtoself,\" \"namedpipe,\" \"namedpipeclient,\" and \"token duplication,\" the rule targets behaviors indicative of attempts to manipulate access tokens or inter-process communication mechanisms, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell).",
  "invoke_privescaudit": "The YARA rule \"invoke_privescaudit\" is designed to detect the use of the \"Invoke-PrivescAudit\" script, which is associated with PowerShell-based privilege escalation auditing, potentially indicating an attempt to identify and exploit privilege escalation vulnerabilities. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, often leveraged by adversaries to gain elevated access or execute malicious payloads.",
  "ps_amsi_bypass_305": "The YARA rule \"ps_amsi_bypass_305\" is designed to detect attempts to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) in PowerShell scripts, a technique commonly used by attackers to evade detection and execute malicious code. This rule is associated with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, highlighting its role in facilitating unauthorized code execution by circumventing security measures.",
  "encoded_reverse_shell_detection": "The YARA rule \"encoded_reverse_shell_detection\" is designed to identify a PowerShell script that constructs a reverse shell by encoding its payload in Base64, a technique commonly associated with obfuscating malicious commands to evade detection. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for executing commands remotely, often used by attackers to gain unauthorized access and control over compromised systems.",
  "ps_amsi_bypass_311": "The YARA rule \"ps_amsi_bypass_311\" is designed to detect attempts to bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) in PowerShell scripts, a technique commonly used by attackers to evade detection and execute malicious code. This behavior is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution.",
  "dcshadow_set_permissions": "The YARA rule \"dcshadow_set_permissions\" is designed to detect the use of the \"set-dcshadowpermissions\" function, which is associated with the DCShadow attack technique, where adversaries leverage tools like Mimikatz to modify Active Directory object permissions stealthily. This rule specifically identifies patterns indicative of unauthorized permission changes, potentially allowing attackers to impersonate domain controllers and manipulate directory data, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell).",
  "invoke_assembly_script": "The YARA rule \"invoke_assembly_script\" is designed to detect the execution of PowerShell scripts that attempt to load and invoke .NET assemblies, which may indicate the use of the \"PowerShell\" technique for executing code via scripting (MITRE ATT&CK T1059.001). This rule identifies specific error messages and loading attempts that suggest potential misuse or failure in executing .NET assemblies, which could be indicative of malicious activity or exploitation attempts.",
  "ps_is_debug_presented": "The YARA rule \"ps_is_debug_presented\" is designed to detect the presence of debugging processes or attempts to identify if a debugger is attached to a system, which is indicative of adversaries employing the PowerShell technique for script-based debugging or analysis, as referenced by MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001. This rule targets strings related to debugging activities, such as \"debuggerprocesses\" and \"system.diagnostics.debugger,\" to identify potential evasion or reverse engineering efforts by threat actors.",
  "ps_rundll32_1141": "The YARA rule \"ps_rundll32_1141\" is designed to detect the use of the \"rundll32\" executable, which is commonly leveraged by adversaries to execute code via DLLs as part of the Command and Scripting Interpreter technique, specifically identified by MITRE ATT&CK as T1059.001. This rule targets the detection of potentially malicious behavior where rundll32 is used to execute scripts or commands, often indicative of code execution or persistence mechanisms employed by threat actors.",
  "generic_powershell_ed3fc_361": "The YARA rule \"generic_powershell_ed3fc_361\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that invoke potentially malicious functions, specifically targeting the \"invoke-sharpprinter\" command, which may be associated with script-based execution techniques under MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001. This rule aims to identify suspicious or unauthorized use of PowerShell for executing code that could facilitate lateral movement or system compromise.",
  "ps_bxor_1133": "The YARA rule \"ps_bxor_1133\" is designed to detect the use of the PowerShell bitwise XOR operator \"-bxor,\" which is often employed in obfuscation techniques to manipulate or decode data in scripts, potentially indicating malicious activity. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, commonly leveraged by threat actors to execute obfuscated or encoded commands.",
  "rdp_connections_history_detection": "The YARA rule \"rdp_connections_history_detection\" is designed to identify artifacts related to Remote Desktop Protocol (RDP) connection history in the Windows registry, which may indicate unauthorized or suspicious remote access activity, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell). This rule detects specific registry keys and strings associated with terminal server client usage, potentially revealing attempts to access or manipulate RDP session data for malicious purposes.",
  "dummy_file_detection": "The YARA rule \"dummy_file_detection\" is designed to identify scripts or files that attempt to disable or bypass the Antimalware Scan Interface (AMSI) by setting its configuration to \"none,\" which is indicative of a technique used in PowerShell scripting attacks to evade detection, corresponding to MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001. Additionally, the presence of the string \"# this is just a dummy\" suggests the rule targets files that may contain placeholder or deceptive comments, potentially used to obfuscate malicious intent.",
  "invoke_wmicommand_detection": "The YARA rule \"invoke_wmicommand_detection\" is designed to identify the use of the \"Invoke-WmiCommand\" PowerShell script, which is associated with the execution of remote commands on Windows systems via Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI), a technique categorized under MITRE ATT&CK ID T1059.001. This detection targets potential malicious behavior where adversaries leverage WMI to execute commands or scripts on remote machines, often as part of lateral movement or remote execution attacks.",
  "malicious_ad_structure_script": "The YARA rule \"malicious_ad_structure_script\" is designed to detect scripts that potentially modify Active Directory structures by creating new organizational units and users, and altering user identities, which aligns with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001 (PowerShell). This behavior may indicate unauthorized administrative actions or privilege escalation attempts within an Active Directory environment.",
  "scoop_checkhash_activity": "The YARA rule \"scoop_checkhash_activity\" is designed to detect the execution of PowerShell scripts associated with the Scoop package manager, specifically identifying commands that manipulate directory paths and arguments, which may indicate suspicious or unauthorized script execution. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, focusing on PowerShell-based command and script execution that could be leveraged for malicious activities such as privilege escalation or lateral movement.",
  "malicious_gppautologon_script": "The YARA rule \"malicious_gppautologon_script\" is designed to detect the use of the \"get-gppautologon\" script, which is associated with the exploitation of Group Policy Preferences (GPP) to retrieve plaintext credentials stored in the \"registry.xml\" file, potentially indicating unauthorized access or lateral movement within a network. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for command and script execution, highlighting a threat related to credential theft and privilege escalation.",
  "ps_clear_eventlog_art": "The YARA rule \"ps_clear_eventlog_art\" is designed to detect the use of PowerShell scripts that clear Windows event logs, a technique associated with adversaries attempting to cover their tracks by removing evidence of their activities. This behavior corresponds to MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, specifically targeting log manipulation to evade detection.",
  "windowsenum_potential_malware": "The YARA rule \"windowsenum_potential_malware\" is designed to detect scripts that perform unauthorized enumeration and information gathering on Windows systems, indicative of malicious reconnaissance activities. It targets the use of PowerShell with execution policy bypass, domain and group enumeration, file system exploration, and registry searches for sensitive information, aligning with the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves command and scripting interpreter abuse.",
  "powershell_maliciousactivity_detection_7e31d": "The YARA rule \"powershell_maliciousactivity_detection_7e31d\" is designed to identify potentially malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize techniques such as loading assemblies from base64-encoded strings and patching methods, indicative of code injection or manipulation activities. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the abuse of PowerShell for executing unauthorized or harmful operations.",
  "ps_obfuscation_300": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_300\" is designed to detect obfuscated PowerShell scripts that utilize the `Invoke-Expression` (`iex`) command in conjunction with the `-join` operator to concatenate numeric arrays into strings, a technique commonly associated with script obfuscation and execution in adversary tactics. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of PowerShell for execution, often employed by threat actors to evade detection and execute malicious payloads.",
  "invoke_netstat": "The YARA rule \"invoke_netstat\" is designed to detect the use of the \"invoke-netstat\" command, which is indicative of adversaries leveraging PowerShell to execute system network diagnostics as part of their reconnaissance activities. This behavior aligns with MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, where attackers utilize scripting to gather information about network connections and potentially identify targets for further exploitation.",
  "as_obfuscation": "The YARA rule \"as_obfuscation\" is designed to detect obfuscated JavaScript code that utilizes string manipulation and randomization techniques to dynamically construct and execute potentially malicious files, such as executables or scripts, which is indicative of code obfuscation techniques associated with scripting languages. The rule targets behaviors related to MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Visual Basic), which involve the use of obfuscation to evade detection and facilitate execution of malicious payloads.",
  "ps_obfuscation_328": "The YARA rule \"ps_obfuscation_328\" is designed to detect obfuscated PowerShell scripts that utilize a specific pattern of concatenated single or double quotes interspersed with lowercase alphabetic characters, indicative of attempts to evade detection mechanisms. This rule targets the MITRE ATT&CK technique T1059.001, which involves the use of command and scripting interpreters for malicious purposes, specifically focusing on obfuscation techniques to conceal script execution.",
  "as_startup_folder_persistence": "The YARA rule \"as_startup_folder_persistence\" is designed to detect malicious scripts or programs that establish persistence by creating shortcuts or saving files in the startup folder, potentially using scripting languages or commands associated with the MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Visual Basic) and T1059.007 (JavaScript/JScript). This behavior indicates an attempt to execute malicious code automatically upon system startup, thereby maintaining persistence on the compromised system.",
  "as_encoding": "The YARA rule \"as_encoding\" is designed to detect the use of encoding functions, specifically base64 and hexadecimal, within scripts or binaries, which are commonly employed by adversaries to obfuscate payloads or commands as part of execution techniques. This rule targets behaviors associated with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (PowerShell), indicating potential script-based execution or command-line obfuscation activities.",
  "as_ransom_note_indication": "The YARA rule \"as_ransom_note_indication\" is designed to detect the presence of ransom notes typically associated with ransomware attacks, characterized by strings such as \"decrypt.txt,\" \"bitcoin,\" and \"files are encrypted,\" which indicate attempts to extort victims by demanding cryptocurrency payments for file decryption. This rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript), suggesting the use of scripting languages in the deployment or execution of ransomware.",
  "as_create_folder": "The YARA rule \"as_create_folder\" is designed to detect the use of scripting techniques that involve the creation of folders via the \"ifilesystem3.createfolder\" method, which is indicative of potentially malicious activity related to command and script execution. This rule targets behaviors associated with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Visual Basic), commonly used for executing scripts that may facilitate unauthorized access or persistence within a system.",
  "as_execution_indication": "The YARA rule \"as_execution_indication\" is designed to detect potential execution of commands or scripts indicative of command and script interpreter abuse, specifically targeting techniques related to Windows Command Shell (T1059.005) and PowerShell (T1059.007). It identifies strings and patterns commonly associated with the execution of shell commands or scripts, which may be used by threat actors to execute malicious payloads or gain unauthorized access to systems.",
  "as_query_fw": "The YARA rule \"as_query_fw\" is designed to detect the presence of scripts or binaries that query or interact with firewall products, potentially indicating attempts to bypass or manipulate firewall settings. This rule is associated with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript), suggesting the use of scripting languages to execute commands that could compromise network security.",
  "as_query_host": "The YARA rule \"as_query_host\" is designed to detect malicious activity involving the querying of system information through Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI), specifically targeting the \"win32_computersystem\" class, which is indicative of adversaries leveraging WMI for system reconnaissance or lateral movement. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript/JScript), highlighting potential abuse of scripting environments to execute WMI queries for gathering host details.",
  "as_query_scheduledjob": "The YARA rule \"as_query_scheduledjob\" is designed to detect the use of Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI) to query or manipulate scheduled jobs, which is indicative of adversaries leveraging WMI for execution or persistence purposes, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (WMI) and T1059.007 (Scheduled Task/Job). This rule specifically targets the presence of the string \"win32_scheduledjob,\" which suggests potential unauthorized or malicious activity involving scheduled tasks on Windows systems.",
  "as_http_download": "The YARA rule \"as_http_download\" is designed to detect scripts or binaries that utilize HTTP request objects, specifically \"iserverxmlhttprequest2.open\" and \"iwinhttprequest.open,\" to perform network communications, potentially indicating the use of scripting techniques for downloading or executing malicious payloads over HTTP. This rule targets behaviors associated with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Visual Basic), which involve the use of scripting languages to facilitate unauthorized data transfer or command execution.",
  "as_dropper_suspicion": "The YARA rule \"as_dropper_suspicion\" is designed to detect potential dropper activity associated with script-based execution and file manipulation, specifically targeting the use of PowerShell and executable files, as indicated by the detection of strings related to file saving operations and suspicious binary patterns. The rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript), suggesting its focus on identifying scripts that may be used to download or execute malicious payloads.",
  "as_runpowershellhttp": "The YARA rule \"as_runpowershellhttp\" is designed to detect malicious scripts that utilize PowerShell to execute HTTP requests via the `IServerXMLHTTPRequest2` object, potentially indicating a technique for remote command execution or data exfiltration over HTTP. This rule targets behaviors associated with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript/JScript), highlighting the use of PowerShell in conjunction with HTTP communication for potentially unauthorized or harmful activities.",
  "as_susp_flow": "The YARA rule \"as_susp_flow\" is designed to detect potentially malicious scripts and activities associated with the execution of Visual Basic Script (.vbs) and PowerShell commands, which may indicate script-based threats or living-off-the-land techniques. Specifically, it targets behaviors related to file system manipulation, script execution, and command-line operations that align with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Visual Basic) and T1059.007 (PowerShell), often used for unauthorized access or persistence in compromised environments.",
  "as_javascript_activex_downloader": "The YARA rule \"as_javascript_activex_downloader\" is designed to detect JavaScript-based malware that utilizes ActiveX objects to perform HTTP GET requests for downloading additional payloads, specifically targeting techniques associated with T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (ActiveX). This rule identifies scripts that manipulate environment variables and use the \"IServerXMLHTTPRequest2\" ActiveX object to execute network operations, indicative of a downloader behavior commonly used in initial access or lateral movement stages of cyber attacks.",
  "as_lolbin": "The YARA rule \"as_lolbin\" is designed to detect the use of living-off-the-land binaries (LOLBins) such as \"powershell,\" \"certutil,\" \"mshta,\" \"rundll32,\" \"bitsadmin,\" \"cmstp.exe,\" \"installutil,\" and \"%comspec%\" that adversaries exploit to execute scripts or commands for malicious purposes, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript/JScript). This rule identifies potential abuse of legitimate system binaries to bypass security controls and execute unauthorized actions within a compromised environment.",
  "as_socgholish": "The YARA rule \"as_socgholish\" is designed to detect malicious scripts associated with the SocGholish malware, which typically employs obfuscated JavaScript to perform drive-by downloads or phishing attacks. The rule identifies specific patterns related to the misuse of the `IServerXMLHTTPRequest2` object for sending encoded data and making HTTP POST requests, indicative of command and control communication or data exfiltration techniques, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (JavaScript/JScript).",
  "as_execcommnadgen_04": "The YARA rule \"as_execcommnadgen_04\" is designed to detect the use of Windows Script Host (WSH) for executing commands, specifically targeting the use of \"iwshshell3.run\" and \"bitsadmin\" for potentially malicious file downloads or command execution, which aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Visual Basic) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript). This rule identifies suspicious activity related to script-based execution and network communication, often indicative of initial access or lateral movement within a compromised environment.",
  "as_registry_persistence": "The YARA rule \"as_registry_persistence\" is designed to detect attempts to establish persistence on a Windows system by monitoring for registry modifications that involve writing to the \"CurrentVersion\\Run\" or \"CurrentVersion\\RunOnce\" keys, which are commonly used by adversaries to execute scripts or programs automatically upon system startup. This rule targets techniques associated with MITRE ATT&CK tactics T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Visual Basic) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript), indicating potential script-based persistence mechanisms.",
  "as_registry_write": "The YARA rule \"as_registry_write\" is designed to detect scripts or binaries that perform registry modifications using Active Scripting techniques, specifically through the use of \".regwrite\" and \"writereg(\" functions. This behavior is associated with the MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Visual Basic) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript/JScript), indicating potential unauthorized or malicious registry changes.",
  "as_process_information": "The YARA rule \"as_process_information\" is designed to detect malicious behavior associated with the manipulation of process startup information, which is indicative of adversaries using scripting techniques to execute or modify processes in a manner consistent with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (PowerShell) and T1059.007 (JavaScript/JScript). This rule targets specific strings related to process creation and management, potentially identifying attempts to execute scripts that alter process attributes for evasion or privilege escalation purposes.",
  "as_wmi_scripting": "The YARA rule \"as_wmi_scripting\" is designed to detect the use of Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI) scripting, specifically targeting the execution of queries via the `iswbemservicesex.execquery` method and the `winmgmts:` namespace, which are indicative of adversaries leveraging WMI for system reconnaissance or lateral movement, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (WMI) and T1059.007 (JavaScript).",
  "as_js_vbs_activex_downloader": "The YARA rule \"as_js_vbs_activex_downloader\" is designed to detect scripts that utilize ActiveX objects to perform malicious activities, specifically downloading and executing files via JavaScript or VBScript. It targets behaviors associated with the use of WScript.Shell, FileSystemObject, and MSXML2.XMLHTTP to facilitate unauthorized file downloads and potential execution of PowerShell scripts, aligning with MITRE techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (VBScript).",
  "as_query_process": "The YARA rule \"as_query_process\" is designed to detect the execution of WMI queries that enumerate running processes on a Windows system, specifically targeting the use of the query \"select * from win32_process.\" This behavior is associated with adversaries leveraging Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI) for system discovery and process enumeration, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Windows Command Shell).",
  "as_execcommnadgen_05": "The YARA rule \"as_execcommnadgen_05\" is designed to detect malicious scripts or executables that utilize Windows scripting techniques to execute commands, specifically targeting behaviors associated with command and script interpreters as outlined in MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Windows Command Shell). The rule identifies patterns indicative of script-based execution, such as object creation for shell access, command execution, and environment variable manipulation, which are commonly used in lateral movement and system exploitation activities.",
  "as_file_copy": "The YARA rule \"as_file_copy\" is designed to detect the use of scripting techniques, specifically through the presence of the string \".copyfile,\" which may indicate the execution of malicious scripts or commands that copy files as part of a broader attack strategy. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Visual Basic) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript), suggesting potential abuse of scripting languages for unauthorized file operations.",
  "as_scheduled_task_creation": "The YARA rule \"as_scheduled_task_creation\" is designed to detect the creation of scheduled tasks using command-line utilities, specifically identifying the use of \"schtasks\" and associated parameters, which may indicate the execution of malicious scripts or commands as part of adversary techniques for persistence or execution (MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 and T1059.007). This rule targets the detection of potential unauthorized task scheduling activities that could be leveraged by attackers to maintain access or automate malicious operations on compromised systems.",
  "as_suspicious_execution": "The YARA rule \"as_suspicious_execution\" is designed to detect potentially malicious execution patterns involving Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI) and scripting interfaces, specifically targeting the use of WMI to create or execute processes and the invocation of scripting engines like WScript and Shell.Application. This rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (WMI) and T1059.007 (JavaScript/JScript), indicating its focus on identifying suspicious script-based execution methods that may be leveraged by adversaries for system exploitation or lateral movement.",
  "as_file_delete": "The YARA rule \"as_file_delete\" is designed to detect the use of scripting techniques, specifically within JavaScript or VBScript environments, that invoke the deletion of files through the \"ifilesystem3.deletefile\" method, which is indicative of potential file manipulation or destruction activities. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (VBScript), highlighting the threat of script-based file deletion operations that may be leveraged by adversaries for malicious purposes such as data destruction or system sabotage.",
  "as_execcommnadgen_06": "The YARA rule \"as_execcommnadgen_06\" is designed to detect malicious activity involving the use of PowerShell scripts to manipulate system folders and execute commands, specifically targeting techniques related to Command and Scripting Interpreter (T1059.005) and PowerShell (T1059.007) as per MITRE ATT&CK framework. The rule identifies patterns indicative of folder creation, folder retrieval, temporary file usage, command execution, and the modification of Microsoft Defender preferences to exclude certain paths, which are common tactics used by adversaries to evade detection and maintain persistence.",
  "as_injection_indication": "The YARA rule \"as_injection_indication\" is designed to detect potential process injection techniques, specifically targeting behaviors associated with code injection into remote processes, as indicated by the presence of functions such as \"createthread,\" \"rtlmovememory,\" \"virtualalloc,\" \"createremotethread,\" and \"virtualprotect.\" These functions are commonly used in adversary techniques for executing arbitrary code within the address space of another process, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript/JScript).",
  "as_execcommnadgen_03": "The YARA rule \"as_execcommnadgen_03\" is designed to detect malicious scripts or binaries that utilize the \"IServerXMLHTTPRequest2\" object to perform HTTP POST requests, potentially indicating the use of scripting for command and control communication or data exfiltration, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Visual Basic). This rule targets the identification of suspicious network activity that may be indicative of remote code execution or unauthorized data transmission.",
  "as_execcommnadgen_02": "The YARA rule \"as_execcommnadgen_02\" is designed to detect malicious scripts that utilize COM objects for executing commands and potentially exfiltrating data over HTTP, specifically targeting techniques associated with MITRE ATT&CK T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Visual Basic) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript). The detection strings indicate the use of COM objects such as `IDictionary` and `IServerXMLHTTPRequest2`, which are commonly leveraged in script-based attacks to manipulate data and perform network communications.",
  "as_downloader_powershell": "The YARA rule \"as_downloader_powershell\" is designed to detect malicious PowerShell scripts that utilize various methods for downloading files from the internet, including the use of System.Net.WebClient, Invoke-WebRequest, and BitsTransfer, which are indicative of the MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Windows Command Shell). This rule targets scripts that may be employed by attackers to download and execute additional payloads, thereby facilitating the initial stages of a cyber attack or malware distribution.",
  "as_execcommnadgen_01": "The YARA rule \"as_execcommnadgen_01\" is designed to detect malicious scripts or executables that utilize Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI) and Windows Script Host (WSH) to execute system commands, specifically targeting techniques associated with Command and Scripting Interpreter (T1059.005) and WMI (T1059.007) for lateral movement or system reconnaissance. The detection strings indicate the rule's focus on identifying scripts that perform network diagnostics via WMI and execute commands through the command prompt, potentially indicating unauthorized system access or control.",
  "as_malware_strings": "The YARA rule \"as_malware_strings\" is designed to detect scripts that utilize Active Scripting components, specifically targeting behaviors associated with file manipulation, registry modification, and script execution, which are indicative of malicious activities such as file system traversal and persistence mechanisms. The rule aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Visual Basic), highlighting its focus on identifying potentially harmful scripts that exploit these scripting languages for unauthorized actions.",
  "as_suspcodeencoded": "The YARA rule \"as_suspcodeencoded\" is designed to detect potentially malicious scripts that utilize Active Scripting techniques, specifically targeting encoded scripts that may leverage VBScript or JScript functionalities for execution, as indicated by the presence of strings related to file operations and data encoding/decoding. The rule is associated with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Visual Basic), suggesting its focus on identifying scripts that may be used for code execution or persistence through encoded payloads.",
  "as_query_av": "The YARA rule \"as_query_av\" is designed to detect the presence of scripts or binaries that query or interact with antivirus products, potentially indicating attempts to evade or disable security software. This behavior aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Visual Basic) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript/JScript), suggesting the use of scripting languages to manipulate or gather information about antivirus solutions.",
  "as_query_network": "The YARA rule \"as_query_network\" is designed to detect the use of Windows Management Instrumentation (WMI) queries targeting network-related classes, specifically \"win32_network\" and \"win32_networkadapterconfiguration,\" which may indicate adversarial attempts to gather network configuration details for reconnaissance or lateral movement. This detection aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: JavaScript/JScript), suggesting potential misuse of scripting environments to execute WMI queries for network discovery.",
  "as_execwin32": "The YARA rule \"as_execwin32\" is designed to detect the execution of Windows processes via the Win32_Process class, specifically targeting command-line execution methods that may involve PowerShell, which aligns with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: PowerShell) and T1059.007 (Command and Scripting Interpreter: Windows Command Shell). This rule identifies potential malicious behavior where adversaries leverage scripting and command-line interfaces to execute arbitrary code or commands on Windows systems.",
  "as_com": "The YARA rule \"as_com\" is designed to detect the use of COM objects for malicious purposes, specifically targeting the instantiation and manipulation of COM objects through the \"dllgetclassobject\" and \"ihost.createobject\" functions, which are indicative of adversaries leveraging scripting techniques for execution (T1059.005) and native API access (T1059.007) to facilitate unauthorized actions or persistence on a system. This rule is aimed at identifying potential abuse of COM technology to execute arbitrary code or gain control over system components.",
  "as_create_text_file": "The YARA rule \"as_create_text_file\" is designed to detect the use of scripting techniques that leverage the FileSystemObject's CreateTextFile method, commonly associated with adversaries executing scripts to create or modify text files on a system. This behavior is indicative of potential malicious activity related to command and script execution, specifically targeting techniques outlined in MITRE ATT&CK T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Visual Basic).",

  "as_shortcut_creation": "The YARA rule \"as_shortcut_creation\" is designed to detect the use of scripting techniques that create shortcuts, potentially indicating the presence of malicious scripts or payloads that leverage shortcut creation for persistence or execution, aligning with MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (Visual Basic).   "as_temp_folder_indication": "The YARA rule \"as_temp_folder_indication\" is designed to detect scripts or executables that utilize temporary folders for potentially malicious activities, specifically targeting techniques related to scripting and command-line interface abuse as indicated by MITRE ATT&CK techniques T1059.005 (JavaScript) and T1059.007 (PowerShell).   "as_malicious_js_dropper": "The YARA rule \"as_malicious_js_dropper\" is designed to detect JavaScript-based droppers that utilize ActiveX objects to download and execute malicious payloads from the internet, specifically targeting techniques related to Command and Scripting Interpreter (T1059.005) and JavaScript (T1059.007) as per MITRE ATT&CK framework.
